.
∎

Finally, we can define the
annotated trace equivalence
and show that,
for action-deterministic configurations, it coincides with trace equivalence.

Definition 36
.

Let
A
and
B
be two configurations.
We have that
A\sqsubseteq_{a}B
if,
for every
A^{\prime}
such that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}
with

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
,
then there exists
B^{\prime}
such that
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}B^{\prime}
, and
\Phi\sim\Psi
.
They are in
annotated trace equivalence
, denoted
A\approx_{a}B
, if

A\sqsubseteq_{a}B
and
B\sqsubseteq_{a}A
.

Lemma 37
.

Let
A
and
B
be two action-deterministic configurations such that
\mathsf{skl}(A)=\mathsf{skl}(B)
.

A\approx B\text{ if, and only if, }A\approx_{a}B

Proof.
Firstly,
A\approx_{a}B
trivially implies
A\approx B
.
For the other direction, we use Lemma
13
to conclude.
∎

Appendix B
Compression

B.1
Reachability

Lemma

(
16
)
.

Let
A
,
A^{\prime}
be two configurations and
\mathsf{tr}
be such that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\;\mathsf{tr}\;$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}
is
complete.
There exists a trace
\mathsf{tr}_{c}
, such that

\lfloor{\mathsf{tr}_{c}}\rfloor
can be obtained from
\mathsf{tr}
by swapping
independent labelled actions, and

\lceil{A}\rceil\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{c}$}}}$}}_{c}\lceil{A^{\prime}}\rceil
.

Proof.
Let
A=(\mathcal{P};\Phi)
be a configuration and

(\mathcal{P};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}
a complete execution.
We proceed by induction on the length of
\mathsf{tr}
, distinguishing two cases.

Case 1.
We first consider the case where there is
at least one process in
\mathcal{P}
that is negative and
non-replicated. Since we are considering a complete execution,
at least one negative action
\alpha
is performed on this process in
\mathsf{tr}
.
This action may be an output, the decomposition of a parallel composition,
or the removal of a zero.
If there are more than one such action, we choose the one that can be performed
using
Neg
,
i.e.,
the one that is minimal according to our arbitrary order on labelled skeletons.
Since our action can be performed initially by our process, and by
well-labelling, the label of the action is independent with all labels of
previously executed actions in
\mathsf{tr}
.
Moreover, there cannot be any second-order dependency between
\alpha

and one of those actions. Indeed, if
\alpha
is an output, no input
performed before
\alpha
is able to use the handle of
\alpha
.
It can thus be swapped before all the others by using Lemma
11
,
obtaining an execution of
trace
\alpha.\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
ending in the same configuration
A^{\prime}
.
The rule
Neg
can be performed in the compressed
semantics to trigger the action
\alpha
, and by induction hypothesis on
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
we can complete our
compressed execution towards
A^{\prime}
.

Case 2.
Otherwise, when
\mathcal{P}
contains only positive or replicated processes,
we must choose one process to focus on, start a positive
phase and execute all its actions until we can finally release the focus.
As long as all processes are positive or replicated, only input or
session actions can be performed. In either case, the action yields a new
process (the continuation of the input, or the new session)
which may be negative or positive.
We define the
positive prefix
of our execution as the prefix of
actions for which all but the last transition yield a positive
process.
It is guaranteed to exist because
A^{\prime}
contains only negative
processes.

The positive prefix is composed only of input and replication actions.
Because session actions are performed by negative processes, and no new
negative process is created in the positive prefix, session actions can
be permuted at the beginning of the prefix thanks to Lemma
11
.
Thus, we assume without loss of
generality that the prefix is composed of session actions, followed by input
actions: we write
\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}_{!}.\mathsf{tr}_{\mathrm{in}}.\mathsf{tr}_{0}
.
In the portion of the execution where inputs of
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathrm{in}}
are performed,
there is an obvious bijective mapping between the processes of any
configuration and its successor, allowing us to follow execution threads,
and to freely permute inputs pertaining to different threads.
Such permutations are made possible by Lemma
11
.
Indeed,
they concern actions that are (i) sequentially independent (
i.e.,
labels are independent)
since two different threads involve actions performed by processes in parallel
and (ii) recipe independent since there is no output action in
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathrm{in}}
.

The last action of the positive prefix releases a negative process

P^{-}
. Let
P
be its antecedent (trough its corresponding thread)
in the configuration obtained after the
execution of
\mathsf{tr}_{!}
. We have that:

A=(\mathcal{P};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{!}$}}}$}}_{a}(\mathcal{P}_{1}\uplus\{P\};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{\mathrm{in}}$}}}$}}_{a}(\mathcal{P}_{2}\uplus\{P^{-}\};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}

Now we can write
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{0}\uplus\{P_{\mathsf{f}}\}

where
P_{\mathsf{f}}
is either
P
or a replicated process that gives rise
to
P
in one transition. By permuting actions pertaining to
P_{\mathsf{f}}

before all others thanks to Lemma
11
and previous remarks,
we obtain an execution of the form

A=(\mathcal{P}_{0}\uplus\{P_{\mathsf{f}}\};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{1}$}}}$}}_{a}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0}\uplus\{P^{-}\};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{2}$}}}$}}_{a}(\mathcal{P}_{2}\uplus\{P^{-}\};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}

where
\mathsf{tr}_{1}.\mathsf{tr}_{2}.\mathsf{tr}_{0}
is a permutation of
\mathsf{tr}
of
independent labelled actions,

\mathsf{tr}_{1}=[\alpha]^{\ell}.\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{1}
,
and

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0}=\mathcal{P}_{0}
when
P_{\mathsf{f}}=P
,
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0}=\mathcal{P}_{0}\uplus\{P_{\mathsf{f}}\}
otherwise.

In the compressed semantics, if we initiate a focus on
P_{\mathsf{f}}
we can execute
the actions of
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
and release the focus when reaching
P^{-}
,
i.e.,
we have that (where
\ell^{\prime}
is the label of
P^{-}
):

(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\mathtt{foc}(\alpha)]^{\ell}$}}}$}}_{c}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{1}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0};P^{-};\Phi)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\mathtt{rel}]^{\ell^{\prime}}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0}\uplus\{P^{-}\};\varnothing;\Phi).

We can conclude by induction hypothesis on
\mathsf{tr}_{2}.\mathsf{tr}_{0}
.
∎

B.2
Equivalence

We prove below the two implications of Theorem
18
,
dealing first with soundness and then
with the more involved completeness result.

Lemma 38

(Soundness)
.

Let
A
and
B
be action-deterministic configurations such that
\mathsf{skl}(A)=\mathsf{skl}(B)
.
We have that
A\approx B
implies
\lceil{A}\rceil\approx_{c}\lceil{B}\rceil
.

Proof.
By symmetry it suffices to show
\lceil{A}\rceil\sqsubseteq_{c}\lceil{B}\rceil
. Consider an execution

\lceil{A}\rceil\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
Thanks to Lemma
15
, we know that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\lfloor{\mathsf{tr}}\rfloor$}}}$}}_{a}\lfloor{A^{\prime}}\rfloor
. Let
\lfloor{\mathsf{tr}}\rfloor=[\alpha_{1}]^{\ell_{1}}\ldots[\alpha_{n}]^{\ell_{n}}
,
and we denote
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
the intermediate configurations
that are reached during this execution. We have that:

A_{0}=A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\alpha_{1}]^{\ell_{1}}$}}}$}}_{a}A_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\alpha_{2}]^{\ell_{2}}$}}}$}}_{a}\ldots\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\alpha_{n}]^{\ell_{n}}$}}}$}}_{a}A_{n}=(\mathcal{P};\Phi_{A}).

Applying Lemma
13
, we
deduce that
B
can perform a very similar execution (same labels,
same actions),
i.e.,

B_{0}=B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\alpha_{1}]^{\ell_{1}}$}}}$}}_{a}B_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\alpha_{2}]^{\ell_{2}}$}}}$}}_{a}\ldots\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$[\alpha_{n}]^{\ell_{n}}$}}}$}}_{a}B_{n}=(\mathcal{Q};\Phi_{B}).

with
\Phi(A_{i})\sim\Phi(B_{i})
and
\mathsf{skl}(A_{i})=\mathsf{skl}(B_{i})
for
0\leq i\leq n
.

Due to this strong symmetry, we are sure that
\lceil{B}\rceil
will be able
to do this execution in the compressed semantics. In particular, the
fact that a given configuration
B_{i}
can start a positive phase or has to release
the focus is determined by the set
\mathsf{skl}(B_{i})=\mathsf{skl}(A_{i})
and the fact that it can keep the focus
on a specific process while performing positive actions can be deduce from labels of
\mathsf{tr}
.
Finally, we have shown that if
A_{i}
can execute an action
\alpha
using
Neg
rule
then
B_{i}
can as well.
The only missing part is about the fact that
Neg
has been made non-branching using
an arbitrary order on labelled skeletons.
Let say we can use
Neg
only for actions
whose skeleton is minimal among others skeletons of available, negative actions.
Using
\mathsf{skl}(A_{i})=\mathsf{skl}(B_{i})
, we easily show that this is symmetric for
A_{i}

and
B_{i}
.
This way, we obtain an execution
\lceil{B}\rceil\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
with

\lfloor{B^{\prime}}\rfloor=B_{n}
.
Finally, we have
\Phi(B^{\prime})=\Phi_{B}\sim\Phi_{A}=\Phi(A^{\prime})
.
∎

Lemma 39
.

Let
A
and
B
be two action-deterministic configurations such that

A\approx_{c}B
. If
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
and
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
for a
labelled trace
\mathsf{tr}
then
A^{\prime}\approx_{c}B^{\prime}
.

Proof.
We assume
A\approx_{c}B
,
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
and
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
for a labelled trace

\mathsf{tr}=\alpha_{1}.\ldots\alpha_{n}
. We shall prove
A^{\prime}\approx_{c}B^{\prime}
.
By symmetry, we show one inclusion. Consider an execution

A^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}A_{2}
such that
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr}_{2})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B^{\prime})=\emptyset
. Let us construct
an execution
B^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{2}
such that
\Phi(A_{2})\sim\Phi(B_{2})
.
Firstly, remark that since
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
,
we have that
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
In order to exploit our hypothesis
A\approx_{c}B
, we shall prove that

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr}.\mathsf{tr}_{2})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
All that remains to show is
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr}_{2})\cap(\mathsf{fc}(B)\backslash\mathsf{fc}(B^{\prime}))=\emptyset
.
This is implied by the fact that channels in
\mathsf{fc}(B)\backslash\mathsf{fc}(B^{\prime})

must occur in
\mathsf{tr}
as bound channels and, because of the execution

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}.\mathsf{tr}_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}A_{2}
, those channels cannot appear in the set
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr}_{2})
.

We have that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}.\mathsf{tr}_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}A_{2}
and
thus by hypothesis,
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}.\mathsf{tr}_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{2}
with

\Phi(B_{2})\sim\Phi(A_{2})
.
Since there can be at most one process in
B

that has a label that matches the label of
\alpha_{1}
,
there is at most one configuration
B_{1}
that satisfies

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\alpha_{1}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{1}
. By iteration, we obtain the unicity
of the configuration
B^{\prime}
satisfying
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
.
We thus have obtained the required execution

B^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 40
.

Let
A
and
B
be two action-deterministic configurations. If
for any complete execution
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}
with
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
,
there exists a trace
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
and an execution
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{a}B^{\prime}
such
that
\Phi(A^{\prime})\sim\Phi(B^{\prime})
, then

A\sqsubseteq_{a}B
.

Proof.
Let
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}$}}}$}}_{a}A_{0}
be an execution of
A
with
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr}_{0})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
Firstly, we complete the latter execution in an arbitrary way
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}

such that any process of
A^{\prime}
is replicated and
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset

(it suffices to choose fresh channels for
B
as well).
By hypothesis, there exists an execution

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}$}}}$}}_{a}B^{\prime}
such that
\Phi(A^{\prime})\sim\Phi(B^{\prime})
.
The latter execution of
B
is thus of the form

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}$}}}$}}_{a}B_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{1}$}}}$}}_{a}B^{\prime}
.
It remains to show that
\Phi(A_{0})\sim\Phi(B_{0})
.
For the sake of contradiction, we assume that
\Phi(B_{0})\sim\Phi(A_{0})

does not hold. In other words, there is a test of equality over

\mathrm{dom}(\Phi(B_{0}))
that holds for
\Phi(A_{0})
and does not for

\Phi(B_{0})
(or the converse).
Since
\mathrm{dom}(\Phi(B_{0}))\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\Phi(B^{\prime}))=\mathrm{dom}(\Phi(A^{\prime}))
,
this same test can be used to
conclude that
\Phi(A^{\prime})\sim\Phi(B^{\prime})
does not hold as well
leading to an absurd conclusion.

∎

Lemma 41

(Completeness)
.

Let
A
and
B
be two action-deterministic configurations satisfying

\mathsf{skl}(A)=\mathsf{skl}(B)
.
Then
\lceil{A}\rceil\approx_{c}\lceil{B}\rceil
implies
A\approx B
.

Proof.
Assume
\lfloor{A}\rfloor\approx_{c}\lfloor{B}\rfloor
,
thanks to Lemma
37
, it suffices to show

A\approx_{a}B
.
Let us show the following intermediate result:

for any complete execution
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}
such that

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
, there is an
execution
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}B^{\prime}
such that
\Phi(A^{\prime})\sim\Phi(B^{\prime})
.

Thanks to Lemma
40
and by symmetry of
\approx_{a}
,
this intermediate result implies the required conclusion
A\approx_{a}B
.

Let
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}A^{\prime}
be a complete execution with
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
We thus have that
A^{\prime}
is initial.
Applying the Lemma
16
, we obtain
a trace
\mathsf{tr}_{c}
such that
\lceil{A}\rceil\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{c}$}}}$}}_{c}\lceil{A^{\prime}}\rceil

and
\lfloor{\mathsf{tr}_{c}}\rfloor
can be obtained from
\mathsf{tr}
by swapping independent actions.
Since we have
\lceil{A}\rceil\approx_{c}\lceil{B}\rceil
, we deduce that

\lceil{B}\rceil\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{c}$}}}$}}_{c}\lceil{B^{\prime}}\rceil
with
\Phi(A^{\prime})\sim\Phi(B^{\prime})
.
Lemma
15
gives us
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\lfloor{\mathsf{tr}_{c}}\rfloor$}}}$}}_{a}B^{\prime}
.
We can now apply Lemma
11
to obtain

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{a}B^{\prime}
and conclude.

∎

Appendix C
Reduction

C.1
Reachability

Lemma

(
21
)
.

Let
A
and
A^{\prime}

be two initial configurations such that

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
.
We have that

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}

for any
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\equiv_{\Phi(A^{\prime})}\mathsf{tr}
.

Proof.
Thanks to Lemma
15
, we have that

\lfloor{A}\rfloor\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\lfloor{\mathsf{tr}}\rfloor$}}}$}}_{a}(\mathcal{P};\Phi)
.
We first prove that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
can be performed using
\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$$}}}$}}_{a}
.
For this, it suffices to establish the implication for each of the two
generators of
\equiv_{\Phi}
.
The first case is given by Lemma
11
.
The second one is a common property of (derivatives of)
the applied
\pi
-calculus that follows from a simple observation
of the transition rules.
Finally, we must prove that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
can be played using
\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$$}}}$}}_{c}
.
Thanks to initiality of
A
and
(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
we know that each
block of
\mathsf{tr}
starts when the configuration is initial and after performing it we get another initial configuration.
This is still true in
\lfloor{A}\rfloor\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\lfloor{\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}}\rfloor$}}}$}}_{a}(\mathcal{P};\Phi)
.
Finally, labels of blocks of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
ensures that a single process is used in a positive
part of any block.
Having proving those two facts, we can easily show that each block of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}

can be performed using
\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$$}}}$}}_{c}
.
∎

For the sake of readability of the following proofs, we now introduce some notations
(where
b_{1},b_{2}
are two blocks and
\Phi
is a frame):

•

we note
b_{1}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{s}b_{2}
when
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are
sequentially independent (
i.e.,
for any
\alpha_{1}\in b_{1}
and

\alpha_{2}\in b_{2}
,
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are sequentially
independent);

•

we note
b_{1}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{d}b_{2}
when
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are
recipe independent (
i.e.,
for any
\alpha_{1}\in b_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}\in b_{2}
,
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are recipe independent);

•

for two traces
\mathsf{tr}=b_{1}\ldots b_{n}
and

\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}=b_{1}^{\prime}\ldots b_{m}^{\prime}
, we note
(\mathsf{tr}=_{\mathsf{E}}\mathsf{tr}^{\prime})\Phi
when
n=m
and
for all
i\in[1;n],

(b_{i}=_{\mathsf{E}}b_{i}^{\prime})\Phi
.

Remark 42
.

Let
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
be any compressed execution.
If
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are two blocks of
\mathsf{tr}
and
a_{1}
(resp.
a_{2})

is the first labelled action of
b_{1}
(resp.
b_{2}
) we have the following:

b_{1}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{s}b_{2}\iff a_{1}\text{ is sequentially independent with }a_{2}.

This is implied by the fact that for any other action
a_{1}^{\prime}
of
b_{1}
, its label has
the label of
a_{1}
as a prefix.

Lemma

(
24
)
.

Let
A
be an initial configuration
and
A^{\prime}=(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
be a configuration such that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
.
We have that
\mathsf{tr}
is
\Phi
-minimal if, and only if,

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{r}A^{\prime}
.

Proof.
Let
A
and
(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
be two configurations such that

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
.

(\Rightarrow
)
We first show that if
\mathsf{tr}
is
\Phi
-minimal, then
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{r}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
by induction on the trace
\mathsf{tr}
. The base
case,
i.e.,

\mathsf{tr}=\epsilon
is straightforward.
Now, assume that
\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b
for some block
b

and
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P}_{0};\varnothing;\Phi_{0})\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathsf{tr}
is
\Phi
-minimal, we also have that
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
is

\Phi_{0}
-minimal and thus we obtain by induction hypothesis that

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}$}}}$}}_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{0};\varnothing;\Phi_{0})
. To conclude,
it remains to show that
{\mathsf{tr}_{0}\triangleright b^{\prime}}
for any
b^{\prime}
such that

(b^{\prime}=_{\mathsf{E}}b)\Phi_{0}
. Assume that it is not the case, this means that
for some
b^{\prime}
such that
(b=_{\mathsf{E}}b^{\prime})\Phi_{0}
, the trace
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
can
be written

\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}.b_{0}.\ldots.b_{n}
with:

b_{i}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}b^{\prime}
and
b_{i}\prec b^{\prime}
for any
i>0
, as well as

b_{0}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}b^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}\prec b_{0}
.

Let
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}=\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}.b^{\prime}.b_{0}\ldots b_{n}
. We have

\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\prec_{\mathsf{lex}}\mathsf{tr}
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\equiv_{\Phi}\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b^{\prime}
, which
contradicts the
\Phi
-minimality of
\mathsf{tr}
.

(\Leftarrow)

Now, we assume that
\mathsf{tr}
is not
\Phi
-minimal, and
we want to establish that
\mathsf{tr}
cannot be executed in the reduced
semantics.
Let
\mathsf{tr}_{m}
be the
\Phi
-minimal trace of the equivalence class of
\mathsf{tr}
.
We have in particular
\mathsf{tr}_{m}\equiv_{\Phi}\mathsf{tr}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{m}\prec_{\mathsf{lex}}\mathsf{tr}
.
Now, we let
\mathsf{tr}_{m}^{s}
(resp.
\mathsf{tr}^{s}
) be the
“trace of labelled skeletons” associated to
\mathsf{tr}_{m}
(resp.
\mathsf{tr}
).
Let
\mathsf{tr}_{0}^{s}
be the longest common prefix of
\mathsf{tr}_{m}^{s}
and
\mathsf{tr}^{s}

and
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
(resp.
\mathsf{tr}_{0}^{\prime}
) be the corresponding prefix of
\mathsf{tr}
(resp.
\mathsf{tr}_{m}
).
We have a decomposition of the form

\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b.\mathsf{tr}_{1}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{m}=\mathsf{tr}_{0}^{\prime}.b_{m}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}^{\prime}
with

(\mathsf{tr}_{0}=_{\mathsf{E}}\mathsf{tr}_{0}^{\prime})\Phi
and
b_{m}\prec b
.
Again, since when dropping recipes, the relation
\equiv_{\Phi}
only swaps
sequentially independent labelled skeletons, block
b_{m}
must have
a counterpart in
\mathsf{tr}
and, more precisely, in
\mathsf{tr}_{1}
. We thus have
a more precise decomposition of
\mathsf{tr}
:

\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}.b_{m}^{\prime}.\mathsf{tr}_{12}
such that
(b_{m}^{\prime}=_{\mathsf{E}}b_{m})\Phi
.

We now show that
b_{m}^{\prime}
cannot
be executed after
\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}
in the reduced semantics
(assuming that the trace has been executed so far in
the reduced semantics).
In other words,
we show that
{\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}\triangleright b_{m}^{\prime}}
does
not

hold.
We have seen that
(b_{m}^{\prime}=_{\mathsf{E}}b_{m})\Phi
and
b_{m}\prec b
so it suffices to
show:

b_{m}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}b_{i}
for any
b_{i}\in b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}

First, we prove
b_{i}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{d}b_{m}
(
i.e.,
they are recipe independent)
for any
b_{i}\in b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}
.
This comes from the fact that
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}.b_{m}.\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathsf{tr}_{m}
is plausible,
and thus the inputs of
b_{m}
only use handles introduced in
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}_{0}
which are the same as those
introduced in
\mathsf{tr}_{0}
. In particular, the inputs of
b_{m}
do not rely
on the handles introduced in
b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}
.
Similarly, using the fact that
\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}.b_{m}^{\prime}.\mathsf{tr}_{12}=\mathsf{tr}
is plausible and

b_{m}^{\prime-}=b_{m}^{-}
,
we deduce that handles of outputs of
b_{m}
are not used in
b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}
.

Second, we show that
b_{i}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{s}b_{m}
(
i.e.,
they are sequentially independent)
for any
b_{i}\in b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}
.
For this, we remark that for any traces
\mathsf{tr}_{1}.b.\mathsf{tr}_{2}\equiv_{\Phi}\mathsf{tr}_{1}^{\prime}.b^{\prime}.\mathsf{tr}_{2}^{\prime}

such that
(b=_{\mathsf{E}}b^{\prime})\Phi
, we have that
b\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{s}b_{s}
for all
b_{s}\in\mathsf{skl}(\mathsf{tr}_{1}^{\prime})\backslash\mathsf{skl}(\mathsf{tr}_{1})
where

\mathsf{skl}(\mathsf{tr})
is the multiset of labelled skeletons of blocks of
\mathsf{tr}
,
and
\backslash
should be read as multiset subtraction.
This can be easily shown by induction on the relation
\equiv_{\Phi}
.
By applying this
helping remark to
\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b_{m}^{\prime}.\mathsf{tr}_{2}^{\prime}\equiv_{\Phi}\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}.b_{m}^{\prime}.\mathsf{tr}_{12}
,
we obtain the required conclusion:
b_{m}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{s}b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}
and thus
b_{m}\mathrel{\text{\textbardbl}}^{s}b.\mathsf{tr}_{11}
.
∎

C.2
Equivalence

Proposition 43
.

For any static equivalent frames
\Phi\sim\Psi
and compressed
traces
\mathsf{tr}
and
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
,
we have that
\mathsf{tr}\equiv_{\Phi}\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
if, and only if,

\mathsf{tr}\equiv_{\Psi}\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
.

Proof.
The two implications are symmetric, we thus only show one implication.
Considering the two generators of
\equiv_{\Phi}
, the only non-trivial
step is to show that
\mathsf{tr}.b_{1}.\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\equiv_{\Psi}\mathsf{tr}.b_{2}.\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}

when
(b_{1}=_{\mathsf{E}}b_{2})\Phi
. But the latter condition, together with

\Phi\sim\Psi
, yields
(b_{1}=_{\mathsf{E}}b_{2})\Psi
which allows us to
conclude.
∎

Lemma 44
.

Let
A
and
B
be two action-deterministic
configurations. If for any complete execution of the form
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)

with
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
,
there exists an execution
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{Q};\varnothing;\Psi)
such that
\Phi\sim\Psi
, then
A\sqsubseteq_{c}B
.

Proof.
Let
A
and
B
be two action-deterministic
configurations, and assume that for any complete execution

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
with
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
,
there exists an execution
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{Q};\varnothing;\Psi)

such that
\Phi\sim\Psi
. Now, we have to establish that
A\sqsubseteq_{c}B
.

Let
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\varnothing;\Phi^{\prime})
be a configuration such that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\varnothing;\Phi^{\prime})
. First,
we can complete this execution to reach a process
(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
such that each process
P\in\mathcal{P}
is
replicated,
i.e.,

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};\varnothing;\Phi^{\prime})\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{+}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
is a complete execution.

Without loss of generality, we can choose
\mathsf{tr}^{+}
so that it satisfies

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr}^{+})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
By hypothesis, we know that there exists an execution
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\mathsf{tr}^{+}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{Q};\varnothing;\Psi)
such that
\Phi\sim\Psi
.
Let
B^{\prime}
be the configuration reached along this execution after the
execution of
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
and
\Psi^{\prime}
its frame.
Similarly to the proof of Lemma
40
, we prove that

\Phi\sim\Psi
implies
\Phi^{\prime}\sim\Psi^{\prime}
.
∎

Theorem

(
26
)
.

Let
A
and
B
be two initial,
action-deterministic configurations.

A\approx_{c}B\mbox{ if, and only if, }A\approx_{r}B

Proof.
We prove the two directions separately.

(
\Rightarrow
)
A\sqsubseteq_{c}B
implies
A\sqsubseteq_{r}B
.

Consider an execution of the form
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{r}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
with
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
Using Lemma
23
, we know that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)
, and Lemma
24
tells us that

\mathsf{tr}
is
\Phi
-minimal. Since
A\sqsubseteq_{c}B
, we deduce that
there exists
(\mathcal{Q};\varnothing;\Psi)
such that:

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{Q};\varnothing;\Psi)
and
\Phi\sim\Psi
.

Now, by Proposition
43
we obtain that
\mathsf{tr}

is also
\Psi
-minimal, and so Lemma
24
tells us
that the execution of
\mathsf{tr}
by
B
can also be performed in
the reduced semantics.

(
\Leftarrow
)
A\sqsubseteq_{r}B
implies
A\sqsubseteq_{c}B
.

Relying on Lemma
44
, it is actually sufficient
to show that for any complete execution

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{P};\varnothing;\Phi)=A^{\prime}

with
\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
, there
exists an execution of the form
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}(\mathcal{Q};\varnothing;\Psi)
such that
\Phi\sim\Psi
.
Note that since the given execution is complete, we have that
A^{\prime}
is initial.
Let
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
be a
\Phi
-minimal trace in the equivalence class of
\mathsf{tr}
.
We have that
A
executes
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
in the reduced semantics, and so for
some
B^{\prime}
we have

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{r}B^{\prime}\mbox{~ and ~}\Phi(B^{\prime})\sim\Phi.

Using Lemma
23
, we obtain the same execution
in the compressed semantics.
Finally, by Proposition
43
we obtain

\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}\equiv_{\Phi(B^{\prime})}\mathsf{tr}
, and by Lemma
21
we
obtain:

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}\mbox{~ and ~}\Phi(B^{\prime})\sim\Phi

∎

Appendix D
Optimization for improper blocks

Between any two initial configurations, the compressed as well as the reduced semantics execute a sequence of actions of the form
of the form

\mathtt{foc}(\alpha).\mathsf{tr}^{+}.\mathtt{rel}.\mathsf{tr}^{-}

where
\mathsf{tr}^{+}
is a (possibly empty)
sequence of input actions, whereas
\mathsf{tr}^{-}
is a (non-empty) sequence
of
\mathtt{out}
,
\mathtt{par}
, and
\mathtt{zero}
actions.
Such sequences are called
blocks
. Now, we also make a
distinction between blocks having a null negative phase (
i.e.,

\mathsf{tr}^{-}=\mathtt{zero}
), and the
others. The former are called
improper blocks
whereas the latter are
called
proper blocks
.
Finally, we say that a trace is
proper
if it contains at most one
improper block and only at the end of the trace.

The idea is that, when checking trace equivalence, we do not have to consider
all possible traces but we can actually restrict to proper ones.
We present below an improved version of the notions of compressed and
reduced equivalence.

D.1
Compression

Definition 45
.

Let
A
and
B
be two configurations. We have that
A\sqsubseteq_{c+i}B
if,
for every
A^{\prime}
such that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
and

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
for some
proper

\mathsf{tr}
,
there exists
B^{\prime}
such that
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
with
\Phi(A^{\prime})\sim\Phi(B^{\prime})
.
We write
A\approx_{c+i}B
, if

A\sqsubseteq_{c+i}B
and
B\sqsubseteq_{c+i}A
.

Operationally, we can obtain
\approx_{c+i}
by adding
a case to the
Release
rule (and constraining the former rule
to not apply in that case):

\begin{array}[]{lc}\mbox{\sc Release${}_{i}$}&(\mathcal{P};[0]^{\ell};\Phi)\xrightarrow{\;[\mathtt{rel}]^{\ell}\;}_{c}(\emptyset;\varnothing;\Phi)\hfil\lx@intercol{\\
}\end{array}

This rule discards exactly the traces containing an improper block
that is not at the end: note that having
0
under focus implies
that the negative part of the block will be restricted to
\mathtt{zero}
.
This is because no negative
actions was available before performing this block and consequently there can
only be a
\mathtt{zero}
in the negative part of the block.

Proposition 46
.

Let
A
and
B
be two initial action-deterministic configurations.

A\approx_{c}B
if, and only, if,
A\approx_{c+i}B

Proof.
The
(\Rightarrow)
direction is trivial. We focus on the other one.
Assume that
A\sqsubseteq_{c+i}B
. Let
A^{\prime}
be such
that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
for some
\mathsf{tr}
such that

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
Let
b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}
be the improper blocks that occur in
\mathsf{tr}
. We
have that
b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}
are pairwise independent. Moreover,
we have that there exists
\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}
made of proper blocks such that

\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}.b_{1}.\ldots.b_{k}
is obtained from
\mathsf{tr}
by swapping independent
blocks, and thus
we have that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}.b_{1}.\ldots.b_{k}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
.
There exist initial configurations

A_{0},A_{1},\ldots,A_{k}
such that

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{c}A_{0}
, and

A_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b_{1}$}}}$}}_{c}A_{1},\;A_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}A_{2},\;\ldots,A_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b_{k}$}}}$}}_{c}A_{k}.

Thanks to our hypothesis, we deduce that there exist configurations
B_{0}
,
B_{1},\ldots,B_{k}
such that

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{0}
with
\Phi(A_{0})\sim\Phi(B_{0})
, and

B_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b_{1}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{1},\;B_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b_{2}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{2},\;\ldots,B_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b_{k}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{k}.

We have also that
\Phi(A_{0})\sim\Phi(B_{0})
.
Since blocks
b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}
are pairwise independent, we deduce that
there exist
B^{\prime}
such that
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}^{\prime}$}}}$}}_{c}B_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
with
\Phi(B^{\prime})=\Phi(B_{0})
.
Then, permutations of blocks can be undone to retrieve
\mathsf{tr}
(since
swapping have been done between independent blocks).
∎

D.2
Reduction

Definition 47
.

Let
A
and
B
be two configurations. We have that
A\sqsubseteq_{r+i}B
if,
for every
A^{\prime}
such that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{r}A^{\prime}
and

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
for some
proper

\mathsf{tr}
,
there exists
B^{\prime}
such that
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{r}B^{\prime}
with
\Phi(A^{\prime})\sim\Phi(B^{\prime})
.
We write
A\approx_{r+i}B
, if

A\sqsubseteq_{r+i}B
and
B\sqsubseteq_{r+i}A
.

Proposition 48
.

Let
A
and
B
be two initial action-deterministic configurations.

A\approx_{r}B
if, and only, if,
A\approx_{r+i}B

Proof.
The (
\Rightarrow
) direction is trivial.
We focus on the other one.
Assume that
A\sqsubseteq_{r+i}B
. Let
A^{\prime}
be such that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{r}A^{\prime}
for some
\mathsf{tr}
such that

\mathsf{bc}(\mathsf{tr})\cap\mathsf{fc}(B)=\emptyset
.
Lemma
23
tells us that
A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}A^{\prime}
, and thanks to Lemma
24
, we have that

\mathsf{tr}
is
\Phi(A^{\prime})
-minimal.

Let
b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}
be the improper blocks that occur in
\mathsf{tr}
.
We have that there exist
\mathsf{tr}_{0},\mathsf{tr}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{tr}_{k}
made of proper blocks such that

\mathsf{tr}=\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b_{1}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}.b_{2}.\ldots\mathsf{tr}_{k-1}.b_{k}.\mathsf{tr}_{k}
. We
have that
b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}
are pairwise independent, and also:

\mathsf{tr}\equiv_{\Phi(A^{\prime})}\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{tr}_{k}.b_{1}.\ldots.b_{k}

Because there are no dependencies between

b_{i}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{j}
for
i<j
, and because the
b_{i}
do not have any output,
we have that

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}.\ldots.\mathsf{tr}_{k}$}}}$}}_{r}A_{0}
, and also that:

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b_{1}$}}}$}}_{r}A_{1}
,

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}.b_{2}$}}}$}}_{r}A_{2}
,

\ldots
,

A\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}.\ldots.\mathsf{tr}_{k-1}.b_{k}$}}}$}}_{r}A_{k}
.

Thanks to our hypothesis, we deduce that there exist
B_{0},B_{1},\ldots,B_{k}
such that

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}.\ldots.\mathsf{tr}_{k}$}}}$}}_{r}B_{0}
with
\Phi(A_{0})\sim\Phi(B_{0})
, and also that:

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.b_{1}$}}}$}}_{r}B_{1}
,

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}.b_{2}$}}}$}}_{r}B_{2}
,

\ldots
,

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}.\ldots.\mathsf{tr}_{k-1}.b_{k}$}}}$}}_{r}B_{k}
.

We deduce that there exists
B^{\prime}
such that

B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}\ldots.\mathsf{tr}_{k}.b_{1}.\ldots.b_{k}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
.
Next, we observe that
\Phi(A^{\prime})=\Phi(A_{0})\sim\Phi(B_{0})=\Phi(B^{\prime})
.
From this we conclude

\mathsf{tr}\equiv_{\Phi(B^{\prime})}\mathsf{tr}_{0}.\mathsf{tr}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{tr}_{k}.b_{1}.\ldots.b_{k}
,
hence
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{c}B^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathsf{tr}
is
\Phi(A^{\prime})
-minimal it is also
\Phi(B^{\prime})
-minimal, and thus
B\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.5pt}[5.0pt]{$\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.2pt}[2.0pt][1.0pt]{\scalebox{0.7}{$\mathsf{tr}$}}}$}}_{r}B^{\prime}

by Lemma
24
.
∎
